Complex.conjCAEdefMathlib.Analysis.Complex.Basic, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
$\operatorname{conjCAE}$ is a continuous algebra isomorphism of the set of complexes and the set of complexes.
Formula
$\operatorname{conjCAE} \in \mathrm{ContinuousAlgEquiv} (\Real , \mathrm{Complex} , \mathrm{Complex})$
French
$\operatorname{conjCAE}$ est un isomorphisme d'algèbres continu de l'ensemble de nombres complexes et l'ensemble de nombres complexes.

Wording

Complex.canonicalFactordefMathlib.Analysis.Complex.CanonicalDecomposition, function or constant
Wording
the canonical factor of w and x (from the name)
English
Let $R$ be a real number. Let $w$ and $x$ be complexes. Then the canonical factor of $w$ and $x$ is a complex defined as the quotient of the difference between the exponentiation of the natural inclusion of the real numbers into the complex numbers of $R$ to $2$ and the product of $\operatorname{starRingEnd}$ applied to the set of complexes and $w$ and $x$ and the product of the natural inclusion of the real numbers into the complex numbers of $R$ and $x - w$.
Formula
$R \in \Real , w , x \in \mathrm{Complex} \vdash \operatorname{canonicalFactor} (R , w , x):= \frac{ \operatorname{ofReal} (R)^ {2}- \operatorname{starRingEnd} (\mathrm{Complex} , w)\times x}{\operatorname{ofReal} (R)\times (x - w)}\in \mathrm{Complex}$
French
Soit $R$ un nombre réel. Soient $w$ et $x$ des nombres complexes. Alors le facteur canonique de $w$ et $x$ est un nombre complexe défini comme le quotient entre la différence entre la puissance du [Dep2Noun] naturel de $R$ en $2$ et le produit de $\operatorname{starRingEnd}$ appliqué à l'ensemble de nombres complexes et $w$ et de $x$ et le produit du [Dep2Noun] naturel de $R$ et de $x - w$.

$z \in \mathrm{Complex} \vdash \operatorname{cos} (z):= \frac{ \operatorname{exp} (z \times \operatorname{I})+ \operatorname{exp} (\operatorname{neg} (z)\times \operatorname{I})}{2}\in \mathrm{Complex}$
French
Soit $z$ un nombre complexe. Alors le cosinus de $z$ est un nombre complexe défini comme le quotient entre la somme de l'exponentielle de $z \times \operatorname{I}$ et de l'exponentielle de $\operatorname{neg} (z)\times \operatorname{I}$ et $2$.

Wording
no wording
English
Let $\mathrm{mk}$ be a function from complexes $z_{1}$ and elements $\mathrm{hz}$ of $\mathrm{Proof}$ applied to the proposition that the norm of $z_{1}$ is less than or equal to $1$ to elements of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $z_{1}$ and $\mathrm{hz}$. Let $z$ be a unit closed disc. Then $\operatorname{casesOn}$ applied to $z_{1}$, $\mathrm{hz}$, $\mathrm{mk}$ and $z$ is an element of $\mathrm{motive}$ applied to $z$ defined as the function that maps $\mathrm{mk}$ and $z$ to $z_{1}$ applied to the coercion of $\mathrm{hz}$, $\mathrm{Complex\_\_UnitClosedDisc\_\_norm\_le\_one}$ applied to $\mathrm{hz}$, $\mathrm{mk}$ and $z$.
Formula
$\mathrm{mk} \in \mathrm{Complex} \rightarrow \mathrm{Proof} (\mathrm{Leq} (\operatorname{norm} (z_{1}), 1))\rightarrow \mathrm{motive} (\operatorname{mk} (z_{1} , \mathrm{hz})), z \in \operatorname{UnitClosedDisc} \vdash \operatorname{casesOn} (z_{1} , \mathrm{hz} , \mathrm{mk} , z):= \lambda \mathrm{mk} , z . z_{1} (\operatorname{coe} (\mathrm{hz}), \mathrm{Complex\_\_UnitClosedDisc\_\_norm\_le\_one} (\mathrm{hz}), \mathrm{mk} , z)\in \mathrm{motive} (z)$
French

Soit $z$ un disque fermé d'unités. UpperHalfPlane.IsBoundedAtImInftydefMathlib.Analysis.Complex.UpperHalfPlane.FunctionsBoundedAtInfty, predicate
Wording
f is bounded at imaginary part infty (from the name)
English
Let $f$ be a function from upper half planes to elements of $x$. Then $f$ is bounded at imaginary part infty, if $\operatorname{BoundedAtFilter}$ holds for $\operatorname{atImInfty}$ and $f$.
Formula
$f \in \mathrm{UpperHalfPlane} \rightarrow x \vdash \operatorname{IsBoundedAtImInfty} (f):\Leftrightarrow \operatorname{BoundedAtFilter} (\operatorname{atImInfty} , f)$

f is a zero at imaginary part infty (from the name)

Wording

the canonical contravariant tensor corresponding to the inner product of E (from the docstring)
English
Let $E$ be a set. Then the canonical contravariant tensor corresponding to the inner product of $E$ is a linear map of the set of tensor products of $E$ and $E$ and $\Real$ defined as the lift of the inner product of $E$.
Formula
$E \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{canonicalContravariantTensor} (E):= \operatorname{lift} (\mathrm{inner\_u2097\_} (E))\in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (\Real), \mathrm{TensorProduct} (\Real , E , E), \Real)$
French
Soit $E$ un ensemble. Alors le tenseur contravariant canonique correspondant au produit intérieur de $E$ est une application linéaire de l'ensemble de produits de tenseurs de $E$ et $E$ et $\Real$ défini comme le relèvement du produit intérieur de $E$.

Inner.innerdefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Defs, function or constant
Wording
no wording
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$ be elements of $E$. Then $\operatorname{inner}$ applied to $x$, $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$ is an element of $x$ defined as $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$, $x_{1}$ and $x_{2}$.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , x_{1} , x_{2} \in E \vdash \operatorname{inner} (x , x_{1} , x_{2}):= \operatorname{proj0} (\mathrm{self} , x_{1} , x_{2})\in x$
French
Soit $x$ un ensemble. Soient $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$ des éléments de $E$. Alors $\operatorname{inner}$ appliqué à $x$, $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$ est un élément de $x$ défini comme $\operatorname{proj0}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{self}$, $x_{1}$ et $x_{2}$.

Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Dual

InnerProductSpace.continuousLinearMapOfBilindefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Dual, function or constant
Wording
the continuous linear map of B (from the name)
English
Let $B$ be a continuous linear map of $E$ and the set of continuous linear maps of $E$ and $x$. Then the continuous linear map of $B$ is a continuous linear map of $E$ and $E$ defined as the composition of the continuous linear map of the continuous linear equivalence of the inverse of the dual of $x$ and $E$ and $B$.
Formula
$B \in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (\mathrm{starRingEnd} (x), E , \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , x))\vdash \operatorname{continuousLinearMapOfBilin} (B):= \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{toContinuousLinearMap} (\operatorname{toContinuousLinearEquiv} (\operatorname{symm} (\operatorname{toDual} (x , E)))), B)\in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , E)$
French
Soit $B$ une application linéaire continue de $E$ et l'ensemble d'applications linéaires continues de $E$ et $x$. Alors l'application linéaire continue de $B$ est une application linéaire continue de $E$ et $E$ défini comme la composition de l'application linéaire continue de l'équivalence linéaire continue de l'inverse du dual de $x$ et $E$ et $B$.
InnerProductSpace.toDualdefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Dual, function or constant
Wording
the dual of x and E (from the name)
English
Let $x$ and $E$ be sets. Then the dual of $x$ and $E$ is a linear isometry equivalence of $E$ and the set of strong duals of $x$ and $E$.
Formula
$x , E \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{toDual} (x , E)\in \mathrm{LinearIsometryEquiv} (\mathrm{starRingEnd} (x), E , \mathrm{StrongDual} (x , E))$
French
Soient $x$ et $E$ des ensembles. Alors le dual de $x$ et $E$ est une équivalence d'isométries linéaire de $E$ et l'ensemble de duaux forts de $x$ et $E$.
InnerProductSpace.toDualMapdefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Dual, function or constant
Wording
the dual map of x and E (from the name)
English
Let $x$ and $E$ be sets. Then the dual map of $x$ and $E$ is a linear isometry of $E$ and the set of strong duals of $x$ and $E$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $\operatorname{toLinearMap}$ applied to the inner product of $x$ and $\mathrm{innerSL\_apply\_norm}$ applied to $x$.
Formula
$x , E \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \operatorname{toDualMap} (x , E):= \operatorname{mk} (\operatorname{toLinearMap} (\mathrm{innerSL} (x)), \mathrm{innerSL\_apply\_norm} (x))\in \mathrm{LinearIsometry} (\mathrm{starRingEnd} (x), E , \mathrm{StrongDual} (x , E))$
French
Soient $x$ et $E$ des ensembles. Alors l'application duale de $x$ et $E$ est une isométrie linéaire de $E$ et l'ensemble de duaux forts de $x$ et $E$ défini comme $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{toLinearMap}$ appliqué à le produit intérieur de $x$ et $\mathrm{innerSL\_apply\_norm}$ appliqué à $x$.

Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.EuclideanDist

Euclidean.balldefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.EuclideanDist, function or constant
Wording
the ball of x3 and r (from the name)
English
Let $x_{3}$ be an element of $E$. Let $r$ be a real number. Then the ball of $x_{3}$ and $r$ is an element of $\wp E$ defined as $\operatorname{ofPred}$ applied to the function that maps $y$ to the proposition that the distance of $y$ and $x_{3}$ is less than $r$.
Formula
$x_{3} \in E , r \in \Real \vdash \operatorname{ball} (x_{3} , r):= \operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda y . \mathrm{Lt} (\operatorname{dist} (y , x_{3}), r))\in \wp E$
French
Soit $x_{3}$ un élément de $E$. Soit $r$ un nombre réel. Alors la boule de $x_{3}$ et $r$ est un élément de $\wp E$ défini comme $\operatorname{ofPred}$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $y$ à la proposition que la distance de $y$ et $x_{3}$ est inférieure à $r$.

Formula
$x_{3} \in E , r \in \Real \vdash \operatorname{closedBall} (x_{3} , r):= \operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda y . \mathrm{Leq} (\operatorname{dist} (y , x_{3}), r))\in \wp E$

Wording

English

Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.GramMatrix

Matrix.gramdefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.GramMatrix, function or constant
Wording
the Gram of x and v (from the name)
English
Let $x$ be a set. Let $v$ be a function from elements of $n$ to elements of $E$. Then the Gram of $x$ and $v$ is a matrix of $n$ and $x$ defined as $\operatorname{of}$ applied to the function that maps $i_{1}$ and $j_{1}$ to $\operatorname{inner}$ applied to $x$, $v$ applied to $i_{1}$ and $v$ applied to $j_{1}$.
Formula
$x \in \mathrm{Set} , v \in n \rightarrow E \vdash \mathrm{Matrix} (n , n , x)$
French
Soit $x$ un ensemble. Soit $v$ une fonction des éléments de $n$ à des éléments de $E$. Alors le Gram de $x$ et $v$ est une matrice de $n$ et $x$ défini comme $\operatorname{of}$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $i_{1}$ et $j_{1}$ à $\operatorname{inner}$ appliqué à $x$, $v$ appliqué à $i_{1}$ et $v$ appliqué à $j_{1}$.

Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Harmonic.Basic

InnerProductSpace.HarmonicAtdefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Harmonic.Basic, predicate
Wording
f is harmonic at x4 (from the name, with words of the lexicon)
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $E$ to elements of $F$. Let $x_{4}$ be an element of $E$. Then $f$ is harmonic at $x_{4}$, if $\mathrm{ContDiffAt}$ holds for $\Real$, $2$, $f$ and $x_{4}$ and $\operatorname{EventuallyEq}$ holds for the neighbourhood filter of $x_{4}$, the Laplacian of $f$ and $0$.
Formula
$f \in E \rightarrow F , x_{4} \in E \vdash \operatorname{HarmonicAt} (f , x_{4}):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{ContDiffAt} (\Real , 2 , f , x_{4})\wedge \operatorname{EventuallyEq} (\mathrm{nhds} (x_{4}), \operatorname{laplacian} (f), 0)$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $E$ à des éléments de $F$. Soit $x_{4}$ un élément de $E$. Alors $f$ est harmonique à $x_{4}$ si $\mathrm{ContDiffAt}$ tient pour $\Real$, $2$, $f$ et $x_{4}$ et $\operatorname{EventuallyEq}$ tient pour le filtre de voisinages de $x_{4}$, le Laplacian de $f$ et $0$.
InnerProductSpace.HarmonicOnNhddefMathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.Harmonic.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $f$ be a function from elements of $E$ to elements of $F$. Let $s$ be an element of $\wp E$. Then $\operatorname{HarmonicOnNhd}$ holds for $f$ and $s$, if for all elements $x_{4}$ of $E$, if $x_{4}$ is an element of $s$, then $f$ is harmonic at $x_{4}$.
Formula
$f \in E \rightarrow F , s \in \wp E \vdash \operatorname{HarmonicOnNhd} (f , s):\Leftrightarrow \forall x_{4} \in E . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {s}{x_{4}}\rightarrow \operatorname{HarmonicAt} (f , x_{4})$
French
Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $E$ à des éléments de $F$. Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp E$. Alors $\operatorname{HarmonicOnNhd}$ tient pour $f$ et $s$ si pour tous les éléments $x_{4}$ de $E$, si $x_{4}$ est un élément de $s$, alors $f$ est harmonique à $x_{4}$.
